[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.1 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '      </>}\r\n      <ThinkPause prompt={lesson.prompt}>\r\n        {lesson.reasoning.map(paragraph => <p key={paragraph}>{paragraph}</p>)}\r\n        <Arrow left={item.left} right={item.right}/>\r\n        <p className="co-readback">{item.readback}</p>\r\n      </ThinkPause>\r\n      {lesson.after?.map(paragraph => <p key={paragraph}>{paragraph}</p>)}\r\n      {lesson.check && <Check {...lesson.check}/>}\r\n      {id === \'group-2\' && item.word === \'only if\' && <DirectionSwitch/>}\r\n    </Section>;\r\n  })}</>;\r\n}\r\n\r\nfunction Fundamentals({ roles = false }: { roles?: boolean }) {\r\n  return <>\r\n    {!roles && <>\r\n      <Section title="One condition tells you something else">\r\n        <p>A <strong>condition</strong> is something that can be true or false of the situation we\'re considering. A door is open. A person has a ticket. An order has been packed. Each gives us an idea whose truth we can ask about.</p>\r\n        <p>A <strong>conditional statement</strong> connects two conditions. It tells us that if one is true, the other has to be true too. It doesn\'t tell us that the first condition actually occurred. It tells us what would follow if it did.</p>\r\n        <p>That distinction matters. You can understand a rule about people who have tickets without knowing whether anyone has a ticket. Later, a fact about one person may give you a way to use the rule.</p>\r\n        <div className="co-intro-relationship"><div><span>One condition is met</span><strong>If this is true...</strong></div><div><span>The statement guarantees another</span><strong>...this has to be true too.</strong></div></div>\r\n        <p>You don\'t need symbols yet. First, understand what the sentence is saying.</p>\r\n      </Section>\r\n      <Section title="Why learn this?" dark>\r\n        <p>On the LSAT, you\'re often deciding whether a conclusion follows from what you\'ve been told. A conditional may supply the connection you need. It can also show you exactly where an argument asks you to accept more than its information supports.</p>\r\n        <p>The important part is the direction. If one condition guarantees another, can we start with the other condition and go back? Another statement might allow that. But the first rule alone may not.</p>\r\n        <p>We\'ll work with one everyday rule and change what we know about a particular order. You\'ll see a conclusion the rule supports and another that would need more information. That distinction helps you check what must be true and decide whether an argument has gone too far.</p>\r\n      </Section>\r\n      <Section title="A guarantee is stronger than a tendency">\r\n        <p>Compare these two statements:</p>\r\n        <div className="co-intro-relationship"><div><span>A tendency</span><strong>Orders marked ready are usually packed.</strong><p>A ready order could be an exception.</p></div><div><span>A conditional rule</span><strong>Every order marked ready has been packed.</strong><p>A ready order cannot be an exception under this rule.</p></div></div>\r\n        <p>Usually leaves room for exceptions. You could find an order marked ready that hasn\'t been packed and still accept the first statement. The second doesn\'t leave that possibility open. Every order with that label has been packed.</p>\r\n        <p>For an LSAT question, take the stated rule as given. A real shop could make a mistake, but we\'re asking what follows from this particular statement. The rule gives us a guarantee within the information we\'re considering.</p>\r\n      </Section>\r\n      <Section title="Read one rule in ordinary English">\r\n        <blockquote>Every order marked ready has been packed.</blockquote>\r\n        <p>There are two complete ideas here: an order is <strong>marked ready</strong>, and an order <strong>has been packed</strong>. The sentence tells us something about every order that meets the first condition.</p>\r\n        <p>Read it this way: <strong>if an order is marked ready, then it has been packed.</strong> We don\'t need to know why the shop uses that label. We need to know what the sentence tells us about an order that has it.</p>\r\n        <p>The rule itself doesn\'t identify a particular ready order. A separate fact can tell us whether one of the conditions is met in a particular case.</p>\r\n        <ThinkPause prompt="Order A is marked ready. What do we now know about it?">\r\n          <p><strong>Order A has been packed.</strong> The rule covers every order marked ready, and the fact tells us that Order A is one of those orders. That\'s the connection. The rule tells us what has to be true of the group; the fact lets us apply it to this case.</p>\r\n          <p>We haven\'t learned that every order in the shop is packed. Our conclusion concerns Order A because that\'s the order the fact tells us about.</p>\r\n        </ThinkPause>\r\n      </Section>\r\n      <Section title="The arrow is a shorter way to write it">\r\n        <p>Now that we understand the sentence, we can give the two conditions shorter labels. The arrow records the connection between them.</p>\r\n        <Arrow left="Marked ready" right="Packed"/>\r\n        <p>Read it from left to right: <strong>if the order is marked ready, then it has been packed.</strong> Knowing the left condition lets us establish the right because every ready order is packed.</p>\r\n        <p>The arrow doesn\'t mean “and then.” It also doesn\'t claim the label causes the packing. We\'re recording what follows logically, not a timeline.</p>\r\n      </Section>\r\n      <Section title="Change the fact, keep the rule">\r\n        <p>Keep the rule exactly the same. This time, the fact tells us about packing instead of the ready label.</p>\r\n        <blockquote>Every order marked ready has been packed.</blockquote>\r\n        <ThinkPause prompt="Order B has been packed. Do we know whether it is marked ready?">\r\n          <p><strong>We don\'t know.</strong> Order B could be marked ready. It could also be packed but not marked ready. Both possibilities fit the statement.</p>\r\n          <p>The rule tells us about every ready order. It hasn\'t told us that every packed order gets that label. To conclude that Order B is ready, we\'d need that additional connection.</p>\r\n        </ThinkPause>\r\n        <p>In the drawing below, ready orders sit inside packed orders because every ready order is packed. A packed order can fit inside or outside the ready group. Use the two facts to compare what each one establishes.</p>\r\n        <SetExplorer/>\r\n      </Section>\r\n      <Section title="Try the same reasoning with a new rule">\r\n        <p>You\'ve used the rule in its stated direction and checked what happens when you start from its result. Try that distinction with a different sentence.</p>\r\n        <Check prompt="Every volunteer wears a badge. Ari wears a badge. Is Ari necessarily a volunteer?" options={[\'Yes, the badge proves it.\', \'No, Ari could have a badge for another reason.\']} correct={1} feedback={[\'You used the rule backward. Every volunteer has a badge, but the sentence does not say everyone with a badge is a volunteer. A staff member wearing one would fit the rule too.\', \'A badge alone leaves volunteer status unknown. The sentence allows a staff member with a badge; it has not restricted badges to volunteers.\']}/>\r\n        <p>A matching result can make the reverse feel plausible. Check whether the sentence actually gave you that direction before accepting it.</p>\r\n        <p>We can now name the two jobs the conditions are doing. One is enough to establish the other. The other is required whenever the first holds.</p>\r\n      </Section>\r\n    </>}\r\n    {roles && <>\r\n      <Section title="Sufficient means enough">\r\n        <p>An order being marked ready is sufficient for it being packed. It\'s enough. Once we know the order is marked ready, this rule gives us what we need to conclude it has been packed.</p>\r\n        <blockquote>Every order marked ready has been packed.</blockquote>\r\n        <p>You don\'t need a second fact about its contents. The rule already tells you what must be true of every order with that label. We call being marked ready the <strong>sufficient condition</strong>. Here, sufficient means enough to establish packing under this particular rule.</p>\r\n        <ThinkPause prompt="Order C is marked ready. Do you need another fact about C to know it has been packed?">\r\n          <p>No. The ready label meets the condition the rule tells us about. The rule and that fact are enough to establish packing.</p>\r\n          <p>This doesn\'t make the label the only possible way to find out that an order is packed. It gives us one way to settle that question.</p>\r\n        </ThinkPause>\r\n      </Section>\r\n      <Section title="Enough does not mean the only way">\r\n        <p>Suppose Order D isn\'t marked ready. We no longer have the fact that allowed us to use the rule in its stated direction. But that doesn\'t settle the packing question.</p>\r\n        <ThinkPause prompt="Order D isn\'t marked ready. Could it still have been packed?">\r\n          <p>Yes. D could be packed without a ready label. It could also be unpacked. Neither possibility creates a ready order that hasn\'t been packed, so both fit the rule.</p>\r\n          <p>The sentence gives us one condition that settles packing. It hasn\'t said packing happens only for those orders. When that sufficient condition isn\'t present, the other condition may still be true.</p>\r\n        </ThinkPause>\r\n        <p>Use a new rule to check whether you can recognize what is enough.</p>\r\n        <Check prompt="Every delivery marked collected has a pickup receipt. What is sufficient here?" options={[\'Marked collected\', \'Has a pickup receipt\']} correct={0} feedback={[\'Marked collected is enough to establish a pickup receipt. The rule tells us what is true of every delivery with that label.\', \'You chose the required result. The sentence gives a receipt to every delivery marked collected; it does not say every delivery with a receipt has that label.\']}/>\r\n      </Section>\r\n      <Section title="Necessary means required">\r\n        <p>Now look at the other condition in the same rule. An order marked ready has to have been packed. Being packed is necessary for being marked ready.</p>\r\n        <p>We call being packed the <strong>necessary condition</strong>. Necessary means required. It names something that has to be true whenever the sufficient condition is true.</p>\r\n        <p>Ask “required for what?” Here, required for an order marked ready. The relationship gives the word its meaning.</p>\r\n        <ThinkPause prompt="Which condition does a ready order have to meet?">\r\n          <p>It has to be packed. Otherwise we\'d have a ready order that wasn\'t packed, which the sentence rules out.</p>\r\n          <p>The ready label is enough to establish packing, and packing is required for every ready order. Those are two views of the same relationship.</p>\r\n        </ThinkPause>\r\n        <div className="co-role-pair"><div><h3>Sufficient</h3><p>Marked ready: enough to establish packing.</p><strong>Goes left</strong></div><div><h3>Necessary</h3><p>Packed: required for every ready order.</p><strong>Goes right</strong></div></div>\r\n        <p>Now compare what each starting fact tells us. Keep the same rule while you switch between the ready label and packing.</p>\r\n        <GateExplorer/>\r\n      </Section>\r\n      <Section title="Having a requirement does not guarantee the result">\r\n        <p>Let\'s use a different requirement.</p>\r\n        <blockquote>Entering the archive requires a pass.</blockquote>\r\n        <p>The sentence tells us what someone needs for entry: a pass. If someone enters the archive, they have that pass. That\'s why has a pass belongs on the required side.</p>\r\n        <Arrow left="Enters the archive" right="Has a pass"/>\r\n        <ThinkPause prompt="Mina has a pass. Does this sentence establish that she entered the archive?">\r\n          <p>No. Mina could have a pass and remain outside. The rule tells us what entry requires; it doesn\'t tell us that every person with a pass enters.</p>\r\n          <p>Necessary doesn\'t mean enough. A requirement can be present while the other condition remains unknown. To conclude Mina entered, we\'d need information establishing that additional connection.</p>\r\n        </ThinkPause>\r\n        <Check prompt="Every finalist receives an invitation. Which requirement does this sentence establish?" options={[\'Receiving an invitation is necessary for being a finalist.\', \'Being a finalist is necessary for receiving an invitation.\']} correct={0} feedback={[\'The sentence gives an invitation to every finalist. That makes receiving one required for finalist status; it does not restrict invitations to finalists.\', "You reversed the requirement. Your choice would mean anyone receiving an invitation has to be a finalist. The sentence allows someone who isn\'t a finalist to receive an invitation too."]}/>\r\n      </Section>\r\n      <Section title="Try the sentence from the inside">\r\n        <p>We can check a proposed arrow by imagining a case in which its left side is true. Read the original sentence and ask what you now have to know about that same case. Your answer should match the right side of the arrow.</p>\r\n        <p>This is a <strong>Personalized If/Then</strong>. Read the rule as if it concerns you or something of yours. The point is to test what the sentence guarantees, not guess what usually happens.</p>\r\n        <blockquote>Every order marked ready has been packed.</blockquote>\r\n        <p>For this rule, imagine an order of yours. Keep the same order in mind on both sides.</p>\r\n        <ThinkPause prompt="If my order is marked ready, what do I know about it?">\r\n          <p>It has been packed. Every order marked ready is packed, and my imagined order has that label. Marked ready → Packed matches what the sentence tells me.</p>\r\n        </ThinkPause>\r\n        <ThinkPause prompt="If my order has been packed, must it be marked ready?">\r\n          <p>No. My order could be packed without a ready label. That fits every ready order is packed because it doesn\'t create a ready order that hasn\'t been packed.</p>\r\n          <p>Packed → Marked ready would add a claim about every packed order. The sentence doesn\'t give me that direction. Trying both readings lets me catch the reverse instead of accepting an arrow just because it looks familiar.</p>\r\n        </ThinkPause>\r\n      </Section>\r\n      <Section title="Read the roles in the relationship">\r\n        <p>In <strong>Marked ready → Packed</strong>, the left condition is sufficient for the right: it\'s enough to establish it. The right condition is necessary for the left: it\'s required whenever the left holds.</p>\r\n        <p>These names describe roles in this rule. A different sentence could give the same idea a different role. Don\'t ask whether a pass is sufficient or necessary by itself. Ask what the complete sentence says having the pass does.</p>\r\n        <p>The next two groups give us ways to recognize these roles in varied wording. We\'ll keep returning to the meaning because an indicator can only help if you know what complete idea it refers to.</p>\r\n      </Section>\r\n    </>}\r\n  </>;\r\n}\r\n\r\nfunction GroupLessons({ id }: { id: string }) {\r\n  const group = GROUPS.find(item => item.id === (id === \'group-1\' ? \'g1\' : \'g2\'))!;\r\n  const first = id === \'group-1\';\r\n  return <>\r\n    <Section title={first ? \'Find the sufficient idea\' : \'Find what is required\'}>\r\n      <p>{first\r\n        ? "A sufficient condition gives you enough information to establish the other condition. Group 1 helps you recognize that job in several kinds of sentence. We\'ll work through the meaning of each expression, then practice finding its complete target."\r\n        : "A necessary condition is something the other condition requires. Group 2 helps us recognize what must be true when that other condition holds. The requirement belongs on the right because the left condition cannot hold without it. Having the requirement, though, may leave the left condition unknown."}</p>\r\n      <div className="co-vocabulary">{group.words.map(word => <span key={word}>{word}</span>)}</div>\r\n      <p>An <strong>indicator</strong> is an expression that helps us recognize the relationship. Its <strong>target</strong> is the complete idea it refers to. Finding that idea matters more than taking the next few words in the sentence.</p>\r\n    </Section>\r\n    <IndicatorSections id={id}/>\r\n    <Section title={first ? \'Find the target, then place it\' : \'Find the requirement, then place it\'}>\r\n      <p>{first\r\n        ? "Add what to the indicator when that gives you a sensible question. If what? All what? The only what? Your answer should be the whole idea the expression refers to. Then check whether that idea really is enough to establish the other condition."\r\n        : "Ask what the indicator refers to. Only what? Only if what? What is required? The target can come before or after the expression, so check its meaning in the complete sentence."}</p>\r\n      <p>This is the <strong>What Test</strong>. The question helps you find the indicator\'s complete target before choosing its side of the arrow. Keep any words that limit which people, objects, places, or occasions the sentence covers. The question finds the idea; the whole sentence tells you what that idea does.</p>\r\n      <p>Work through the expressions below. First choose the complete target. Then place it on its side of the arrow and read the relationship in ordinary English.</p>\r\n      <TranslationLab id={id}/>\r\n    </Section>\r\n    <Section title="Keep the whole condition" dark>\r\n      <blockquote>{first ? \'Any applicant who submits late pays a fee.\' : \'Entry requires a current pass.\'}</blockquote>\r\n      <p>{first\r\n        ? "Applicant alone isn\'t the condition. The sentence is about an applicant who submits late. Keep that restriction when you shorten the idea; otherwise your diagram would make a claim about on-time applicants too."\r\n        : "A current pass is required. Has a pass drops current and would include an expired pass. Shortening a label should save writing while preserving what the sentence requires."}</p>\r\n      <Check prompt={first ? \'What goes left in this rule?\' : \'What goes right in this rule?\'} options={first ? [\'Applicant\', \'Applicant submits late\'] : [\'Has current pass\', \'Has any pass\']} correct={first ? 1 : 0} feedback={first\r\n        ? [\'You dropped the late-submission restriction. Applicant alone would cover every applicant, including someone who submits on time. The sufficient idea is applicant submits late.\', \'This keeps the complete sufficient idea. The rule covers applicants who submit late, so keep that restriction with the applicant.\']\r\n        : [\'This keeps the full requirement. Entry needs a current pass; an expired pass does not meet that requirement.\', \'You dropped current from the requirement. Has any pass would include an expired pass, which is not what the sentence requires.\']}/>\r\n    </Section>\r\n    <Section title={first ? \'Read the only as a complete expression\' : \'Compare only with the only\'}>\r\n      {first ? <>\r\n        <blockquote>The only people admitted are members.</blockquote>\r\n        <p>The only what? The only people admitted. The complete target is admitted people. If someone is admitted, the sentence tells us they\'re a member.</p>\r\n        <Arrow left="Admitted" right="Member"/>\r\n        <p>A member who stays outside fits this rule. Nothing says every member is admitted. Check that your diagram keeps that possibility open.</p>\r\n        <Check prompt="The only vehicles permitted inside are electric. Which diagram preserves the rule?" options={[\'Electric → Permitted inside\', \'Permitted inside → Electric\']} correct={1} feedback={[\'You made electric status enough for permission. The sentence requires permitted vehicles to be electric; it does not promise admission to every electric vehicle.\', \'Permitted vehicles have to be electric. This direction preserves the limit on permitted vehicles without adding a promise about all electric vehicles.\']}/>\r\n      </> : <>\r\n        <p>Try the distinction on the order rule we already understand. The wording changes while the connection stays the same.</p>\r\n        <div className="co-word-comparison"><div><blockquote>Only packed orders are marked ready.</blockquote><p>Only refers to packed orders. Packing is required for the ready label, so packed goes right.</p></div><div><blockquote>The only orders marked ready are packed orders.</blockquote><p>The only refers to orders marked ready. That label is enough to establish packing, so marked ready goes left.</p></div></div>\r\n        <Arrow left="Marked ready" right="Packed"/>\r\n        <p>Both sentences preserve the same direction. An order that is packed but not marked ready fits both. Neither sentence makes packing alone enough for the label.</p>\r\n        <Check prompt="Only registered guests receive wristbands. What follows from receiving a wristband?" options={[\'The person is a registered guest.\', \'Nothing about registration.\']} correct={0} feedback={[\'Only limits wristbands to registered guests, so receiving one establishes that the person is registered.\', \'You treated the sentence like all registered guests receive wristbands. Only restricts who receives one, so the wristband does establish that the person is a registered guest.\']}/>\r\n      </>}\r\n    </Section>\r\n    <Section title="From sentence to diagram">\r\n      <p>You have seen the expressions with help. Now use a new sentence. Identify both complete ideas, choose the sufficient one for the left, and check whether your diagram says what the sentence says.</p>\r\n      <p>If you know the meaning but hesitate over the arrow, imagine the left condition is true. Does the sentence then require the right condition? Read the whole relationship in English. If it makes a promise the original didn\'t make, change the direction.</p>\r\n      <DiagramBuilder id={id}/>\r\n      <details className="co-practical-note"><summary>Why write a diagram when I understand the sentence?</summary><p>For one short sentence, you may be able to hold the connection in your head. Here, writing it makes your direction and complete conditions visible so you can check them. If your first diagram doesn\'t match the meaning, find the exact phrase you misread and revise that part.</p></details>\r\n    </Section>\r\n    <Section title="Say the rule without the scaffold">\r\n      <p>Read the new sentence and explain its direction before revealing. If you hesitate, ask what the indicator refers to, then try the proposed relationship in ordinary English. Compare your explanation with the one that appears.</p>\r\n      <Flashcards id={id}/>\r\n    </Section>\r\n    <Section title="Check the meaning, not just the word">\r\n      <Check prompt={first ? \'A report is reviewed if submitted before noon. Which is the same rule?\' : \'A student graduates only if the project is completed. Which is the same rule?\'} options={first ? [\'If reviewed, it was submitted before noon.\', \'If submitted before noon, it is reviewed.\'] : [\'If the student graduates, the project was completed.\', \'If the project is completed, the student graduates.\']} correct={first ? 1 : 0} feedback={first\r\n        ? [\'You reversed the if relationship. Submission before noon is enough for review; the sentence has not said review proves when the report was submitted.\', \'This preserves the if-clause as the sufficient condition. Submission before noon gives us review even though it appears second in the original sentence.\']\r\n        : [\'This preserves project completion as a requirement for graduation. It does not turn completing the project into a promise of graduation.\', \'You turned the only-if requirement into a guarantee. Graduation requires completion, but the sentence allows a completed project without graduation.\']}/>\r\n      <p>The guided practice asks you to write your reasoning with the method beside you. Then the attached drill gives you fresh questions. Use the explanation to inspect the direction and complete conditions, not only whether you chose the same answer.</p>\r\n    </Section>\r\n  </>;\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport function ConditionalOpeningLesson({ unit, onComplete, continueHref, continueLabel = \'Continue to guided practice\', previousHref, reviewed }: ConditionalLessonNoteProps) {\r\n  const [motionPaused, setMotionPaused] = useState(false);\r\n  const group = unit.id === \'group-1\' || unit.id === \'group-2\';\r\n  const orientation = unit.id === \'orientation\';\r\n  const title = group ? unit.id === \'group-1\' ? \'Group 1: what is enough?\' : \'Group 2: what is required?\' : orientation ? \'What are conditionals?\' : \'What is enough, and what is required?\';\r\n  const repairTitle = orientation ? "Don\'t turn a rule into a case fact" : unit.id === \'sufficient-necessary\' ? "Don\'t turn a requirement into a guarantee" : unit.id === \'group-1\' ? \'Keep the direction and the whole condition\' : \'Keep requirements and completed actions distinct\';\r\n  const subtitle = orientation\r\n    ? "A conditional tells us that if one thing is true, something else has to be true too. Let\'s see what that connection lets you conclude, and what it leaves open."\r\n    : group ? unit.id === \'group-1\'\r\n      ? \'These expressions can point to the sufficient idea. Read what each one says before deciding which complete condition belongs on the left.\'\r\n      : \'These expressions can identify a requirement. Find the complete idea the sentence says you need, then check what its presence does and does not prove.\'\r\n    : "The same rule gives its two conditions different jobs. Let\'s take each one seriously before giving it a place in the diagram.";\r\n  return <div className="conditional-note co-opening" data-motion-paused={motionPaused}>\r\n    <DynamicIslandTOC key={unit.id} selector=".co-opening [data-toc]"/>\r\n    <header className={\'co-hero \' + (orientation ? \'co-intro-hero\' : \'\')}>\r\n      <p className="co-kicker">Conditional logic</p><h1>{title}</h1><p>{subtitle}</p>\r\n      <a href="#co-reading">{orientation ? \'Start with the meaning\' : \'Begin reading\'} <span aria-hidden="true">↓</span></a>\r\n      <button type="button" className="co-motion-toggle" aria-pressed={motionPaused} onClick={() => setMotionPaused(!motionPaused)}>{motionPaused ? \'Resume motion\' : \'Pause motion\'}</button>\r\n      {!orientation && <div className="co-hero-orbit" aria-hidden="true"><span>Enough</span><i>→</i><span>Required</span></div>}\r\n    </header>\r\n    <article id="co-reading">\r\n      {group ? <GroupLessons id={unit.id}/> : <Fundamentals roles={unit.id === \'sufficient-necessary\'}/>}\r\n      <ConditionalOpeningMedia unitId={unit.id}/>\r\n      <Section id="repair" title={repairTitle}>\r\n        <div className="co-word-comparison"><div><h3>The tempting move</h3><p>{unit.commonMistake.mistake}</p></div><div><h3>Read the sentence again</h3><p>{unit.commonMistake.repair}</p></div></div>\r\n      </Section>\r\n      <footer className="co-footer">\r\n        <p>{reviewed ? \'You marked this lesson as read. You can return to the examples whenever you need.\' : \'Marking this lesson as read records review. Your practice gives you a chance to use the reasoning yourself.\'}</p>\r\n        <div className="co-choices">{previousHref && <Link to={previousHref}>Previous lesson</Link>}<button type="button" onClick={onComplete}>{reviewed ? \'Lesson read\' : \'Mark lesson as read\'}</button><Link className="co-primary" to={continueHref}>{continueLabel} <span aria-hidden="true">→</span></Link></div>\r\n      </footer>\r\n    </article>\r\n  </div>;\r\n}\r\n'}]

## Tool: custom_tool_call_output

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 5.5 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': "| Word or phrase function | Source tendency | Writing instruction |\r\n|---|---|---|\r\n| Certainty | must, has to, necessary, no way around | Preserve the exact logical strength, then demonstrate it. |\r\n| Possibility | might, could, may, we don't know | Say what remains open and why the rule leaves it open. |\r\n| Drawing a consequence | so, that means, that implies, this tells me | Put the actual reason immediately before the consequence. |\r\n| Giving a reason | because, since | Explain the matching relationship, not merely “because that's the rule.” |\r\n| Inviting attention | let's look, notice, check, imagine | Name the thing to inspect or the fact to change. |\r\n| Naming a boundary | but, that said, sometimes, unless | Keep the qualification beside the advice it limits. |\r\n| Recovering context | so far, return to, now that | Remind the reader of the specific idea the new step depends on. |\r\n| Testing an interpretation | this fits, does that follow, what would I need | Compare the proposed meaning with the whole original sentence. |\r\n\r\nDo not replace natural speech with “identify the guaranteed outcome,” “activate the sufficient trigger,” or “execute the conditional reasoning procedure” when the learner needs an explanation. Technical wording earns its place by helping them think.\r\n\r\n### Technical names arrive at the useful moment\r\n\r\nThe source gives a simple relationship before dissecting sufficient and necessary. It explains the contrapositive in ordinary terms before presenting the flip-and-negate shortcut. It explains why symbols help before building the first diagram. The label arrives when there is a concrete idea to attach it to.\r\n\r\nThis does not mean withholding a definition for suspense. Start with what the topic is. Then give the learner an experience that makes its vocabulary meaningful. A name should settle a distinction the reader has seen.\r\n\r\n### Small verbal irregularities create a person\r\n\r\nThe source sometimes says “little squiggle,” “little bracket cage,” “for real,” or “a bunch of different ways.” It also occasionally uses elevated words such as “ballyhoo” or “cumbersome.” This combination makes the prose human without eliminating precision.\r\n\r\nFor our material, choose the natural word for the situation. Do not manufacture slang or rare vocabulary to imitate personality. Do not import profanity, dismissive reactions, or author-specific tics. The recognizable quality should be a teacher's thoughtful judgment, not a checklist of eccentric phrases.\r\n\r\n## Questions that make the reader think\r\n\r\nQuestions are one of the source's main ways of controlling pace. They have several distinct functions. Choose the function before writing the question.\r\n\r\n| Kind of question | What the source does | What our version should do |\r\n|---|---|---|\r\n| **Prediction before a reveal** | Coffee/walking thought experiment asks what the reader knows, gives a blank, and says STOP. | Ask a bounded question with enough information to answer. Wait for an attempt before showing the reasoning. |\r\n| **One step inside a worked example** | Asks whether the sufficient condition could occur without the necessary burden. | Let the reader see the missing connection, then answer it in the walkthrough. |\r\n| **Meaning check** | Personalized if/then asks what the speaker knows in a proposed situation. | Read the original sentence naturally and test whether the proposed diagram preserves it. |\r\n| **Tempting reverse** | Tries the wrong sufficient condition and shows the interpretation failing. | Make the attractive wrong choice visible without ridiculing it. Explain the unsupported inference. |\r\n| **Reader objection** | FAQ voices worry about time, confidence, and whether to write. | Use the learner's actual objection, then explain the decision criterion. |\r\n| **Notation clarification** | Asks about parentheses and abbreviations. | Answer small questions where the notation first becomes relevant. |\r\n| **Reason for practice** | Explains why the drill asks for written contrapositives. | Say what ability the task checks and why practice may be more explicit than test-day work. |\r\n| **Boundary test** | Four-situation chart asks what has to happen in each case. | Compare the strongest justified answer with what could still be true. |\r\n\r\nAn exposition question can receive an immediate answer. A student question intended to train reasoning needs an actual pause. Do not ask “What do you think?” and reveal the answer in the same breath. Do not add a rhetorical question to every paragraph merely to sound interactive.\r\n\r\nA useful check has three parts: a precise prompt, a learner decision, and a reasoned explanation. For example:\r\n\r\n> **Rule:** Every blue-sticker parcel has been inspected.\r\n>\r\n> **Fact:** This parcel has been inspected.\r\n>\r\n> Does the parcel have to have a blue sticker? Explain what the rule says about that direction.\r\n\r\nThe feedback should identify the gap: the rule covers blue-sticker parcels; it hasn't said all inspected parcels have a blue sticker. “Incorrect. Review sufficient and necessary” is too thin.\r\n\r\n## Transitions explain why the next move follows\r\n\r\nThe source's transitions keep the learner oriented. “So far” recovers what is established. “Let's return” reuses an anchor. “Now that we have a handle on” names the prerequisite for notation. “This leads me to the next question” connects a practical worry to its answer.\r\n\r\nA transition should answer one of these:\r\n\r\n1. What have we established that the next idea uses?\r\n2. What is still unclear?\r\n3. What will we change while holding the rule fixed?\r\n4. Why is the next representation useful now?\r\n\r\nOriginal transitions:\r\n\r\n> We know what the sticker tells us. Now let's see what the inspection stamp tells us.\r\n>\r\n> These sentences use different words, but they describe the same relationship. Let's write that relationship once.\r\n>\r\n> The arrow is pointing the right way. Check one more thing: did we keep the whole condition?\r\n\r\nDo not fill the gap with a curriculum announcement such as “Next, we will explore necessary conditions.” Say why the requirement is the next thing worth examining.\r\n\r\n## Repetition changes the reader's view\r\n\r\nThe breathing/air anchor appears in the opening, indicator families, contrapositive, fact chart, diagram, and target analysis. The reader keeps the same relationship while the representation changes. That saves the effort of understanding a new scenario at every turn.\r\n\r\nWithin the contrapositive explanation, the source repeats the requirement in several forms: air is needed; breathing has to have air; without air the requirement is missing; the original condition cannot hold. Each repetition supplies a step or a new angle.\r\n\r\nFor our writing, reuse an anchor while doing different work:\r\n\r\n1. Read the ordinary meaning.\r\n2. Apply it to a case.\r\n3. Identify the sufficient and necessary ideas.\r\n4. Compare an unsupported reverse.\r\n5. Reword the same relationship.\r\n6. Show the diagram after its meaning is understood.\r\n\r\nIntroduce a fresh example for independent practice. The learner should not be able to answer only because the lesson already solved that sentence.\r\n\r\nA repeated “Keep this” slogan without new reasoning is not explanatory repetition. Nor is rendering the same instructions twice in different panels. See the demonstrated corrections in [COPY-CORRECTIONS](../wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md).\r\n\r\n## Metaphor gives the reader a usable handle\r\n\r\nThe source uses doors, families, activation, burdens, brackets, and putting yourself in a condition's shoes. Each metaphor gives an abstract operation a physical or social handle. A small set of terms recurs, so the reader can keep using it.\r\n\r\nTransfer the method of choosing a metaphor, not the particular inventory. Prefer a familiar image that helps answer a logical question. Explain its literal meaning immediately. Remove it when it adds more things to remember than it clarifies.\r\n\r\nFor instance, a ticket can illustrate a requirement only if the rule is stated carefully. “A ticket is required to enter” does not say a ticket guarantees entry. An analogy fails if it smuggles in that reverse.\r\n\r\nDo not build a competing metaphor for every sentence. Do not invent branded names for every skill. The standard technical terms remain the stable vocabulary.\r\n\r\n## Humor reduces tension without changing the rule\r\n\r\nThe source's coffee obsession makes an unusual absolute rule memorable. Its reluctant-student aside acknowledges the experience of being asked to guess. The imagined green checkmark gives the reader's desire for certainty a recognizable shape.\r\n\r\nHumor can occur inside an explanation when it makes the thought easier to understand. Joshua's source selection supersedes the earlier blanket restriction that wit must stay outside the explanation. It does not authorize mockery, forced slang, exaggerated praise, or an expanding comic story.\r\n\r\nUse a light aside only when it helps the reader inhabit a situation or relax around a specific mistake. Keep it optional. A paragraph should still teach clearly if the aside is removed. Do not use the source's distinctive jokes as templates to be copied.\r\n\r\nNever turn first-person source anecdotes into Joshua's biography. “I ask every student this” requires that Joshua actually does. “Students always learn better this way” requires evidence or a narrower, truthful statement.\r\n\r\n## Emphasis and visuals identify the decision\r\n\r\nBold in the source often marks the rule, an inference, a warning, or an instruction. Italics distinguish examples and sometimes emphasize a word that changes the contrast. A table lets the reader compare controlled variations. A STOP asks the reader to attempt something. A side note answers a local question without interrupting the main line for everyone.\r\n"}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'import { cleanup, fireEvent, render, screen, within } from \'@testing-library/react\';\r\nimport { MemoryRouter } from \'react-router-dom\';\r\nimport { afterEach, expect, it, vi } from \'vitest\';\r\nimport { conditionalUnits } from \'../content\';\r\nimport { ConditionalLessonNote } from \'./ConditionalLessonNote\';\r\nimport { diagramExamples, orderedConditionalIdeas, translationExamples } from \'./ConditionalOpeningLesson\';\r\n\r\nvi.mock(\'@/components/ui/dynamic-island-toc\', () => ({ DynamicIslandTOC: () => null }));\r\nafterEach(cleanup);\r\n\r\nfunction mount(id: string) {\r\n  const unit = conditionalUnits.find(item => item.id === id)!;\r\n  const complete = vi.fn();\r\n  return { complete, ...render(<MemoryRouter><ConditionalLessonNote unit={unit} onComplete={complete} reviewed={false} continueHref="/guided" /></MemoryRouter>) };\r\n}\r\n\r\nfunction choiceOrder(lab: HTMLElement) {\r\n  return [...lab.querySelector(\'.co-choices\')!.querySelectorAll(\'button\')].map(button => button.textContent);\r\n}\r\n\r\nit(\'orders complete ideas consistently while varying the correct position within every group\', () => {\r\n  for (const id of [\'group-1\', \'group-2\']) {\r\n    const targetPositions = new Set<number>();\r\n    const leftPositions = new Set<number>();\r\n    for (const item of translationExamples[id]) {\r\n      const before = { ...item };\r\n      const order = orderedConditionalIdeas(item);\r\n      expect(new Set(order)).toEqual(new Set([item.target, item.other]));\r\n      expect(orderedConditionalIdeas(item)).toEqual(order);\r\n      expect(orderedConditionalIdeas({ ...item })).toEqual(order);\r\n      expect(item).toEqual(before);\r\n      targetPositions.add(order.indexOf(item.target));\r\n    }\r\n    for (const item of diagramExamples[id]) leftPositions.add(orderedConditionalIdeas(item).indexOf(item.left));\r\n    expect(targetPositions).toEqual(new Set([0, 1]));\r\n    expect(leftPositions).toEqual(new Set([0, 1]));\r\n  }\r\n});\r\n\r\nit.each([\'group-1\', \'group-2\'])(\'keeps %s target choices stable while learners change their answers\', id => {\r\n  const { complete } = mount(id);\r\n  const lab = screen.getByText(\'Read the whole idea\').closest<HTMLElement>(\'.co-lab\')!;\r\n  const correctPositions = new Set<number>();\r\n  for (const item of translationExamples[id]) {\r\n    fireEvent.click(within(lab).getByRole(\'button\', { name: item.word }));\r\n    const order = choiceOrder(lab);\r\n    expect(order).toEqual(orderedConditionalIdeas(item));\r\n    correctPositions.add(order.indexOf(item.target));\r\n    fireEvent.click(within(lab).getByRole(\'button\', { name: item.other }));\r\n    expect(choiceOrder(lab)).toEqual(order);\r\n    fireEvent.click(within(lab).getByRole(\'button\', { name: item.target }));\r\n    expect(choiceOrder(lab)).toEqual(order);\r\n  }\r\n  expect(correctPositions).toEqual(new Set([0, 1]));\r\n  expect(complete).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\r\n});\r\n\r\nit.each([\'group-1\', \'group-2\'])(\'varies %s diagram choices and explains the promise each reversed arrow adds\', id => {\r\n  const { complete } = mount(id);\r\n  const lab = screen.getByText(\'Build the diagram\').closest<HTMLElement>(\'.co-lab\')!;\r\n  const correctPositions = new Set<number>();\r\n  for (const [index, item] of diagramExamples[id].entries()) {\r\n    expect(within(lab).getByText(item.sentence)).toBeTruthy();\r\n    const order = choiceOrder(lab);\r\n    expect(order).toEqual(orderedConditionalIdeas(item));\r\n    correctPositions.add(order.indexOf(item.left));\r\n    fireEvent.click(within(lab).getByRole(\'button\', { name: item.right }));\r\n    fireEvent.click(within(lab).getByRole(\'button\', { name: \'Check my diagram\' }));\r\n    expect(within(lab).getByText(item.reversedPromise)).toBeTruthy();\r\n    expect(choiceOrder(lab)).toEqual(order);\r\n    if (index < diagramExamples[id].length - 1) fireEvent.click(within(lab).getByRole(\'button\', { name: \'Next sentence\' }));\r\n  }\r\n  expect(correctPositions).toEqual(new Set([0, 1]));\r\n  expect(complete).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\r\n});\r\n\r\nit(\'teaches inquiry and single-occasion wording before testing a recurring rule\', () => {\r\n  const { container, complete } = mount(\'group-1\');\r\n  const ifSection = container.querySelector<HTMLElement>(\'#co-if-the-starting-condition-can-come-last\')!;\r\n  const whenSection = container.querySelector<HTMLElement>(\'#co-when-an-occasion-can-supply-enough-information\')!;\r\n  const lab = screen.getByText(\'Read the whole idea\').closest<HTMLElement>(\'.co-lab\')!;\r\n  expect(ifSection.compareDocumentPosition(lab) & Node.DOCUMENT_POSITION_FOLLOWING).toBeTruthy();\r\n  expect(whenSection.compareDocumentPosition(lab) & Node.DOCUMENT_POSITION_FOLLOWING).toBeTruthy();\r\n  expect(within(ifSection).getByText(/You can read asked if as asked whether/)).toBeTruthy();\r\n  expect(within(whenSection).getByText(/That reports what was true on one visit/)).toBeTruthy();\r\n  expect(within(whenSection).getByText(/Whenever can express the same recurring relationship/)).toBeTruthy();\r\n\r\n  fireEvent.click(within(ifSection).getByRole(\'button\', { name: \'A locked drawer guarantees an inquiry.\' }));\r\n  expect(within(ifSection).getByText(/You changed a report of a question into a rule about locked drawers/)).toBeTruthy();\r\n  fireEvent.click(within(ifSection).getByRole(\'button\', { name: \'The drawer was locked.\' }));\r\n  expect(within(ifSection).getByText(/You treated the question as if it supplied its own answer/)).toBeTruthy();\r\n  fireEvent.click(within(ifSection).getByRole(\'button\', { name: \'The archivist asked whether the drawer was locked.\' }));\r\n  expect(within(ifSection).getByText(/This preserves the inquiry/)).toBeTruthy();\r\n\r\n  fireEvent.click(within(whenSection).getByRole(\'button\', { name: \'When I checked the gallery, a lamp was on.\' }));\r\n  expect(within(whenSection).getByText(/You treated one reported visit as a promise about every visit/)).toBeTruthy();\r\n  fireEvent.click(within(whenSection).getByRole(\'button\', { name: \'Whenever the alarm sounds, the staff evacuate.\' }));\r\n  expect(within(whenSection).getByText(/Whenever states the recurring connection here/)).toBeTruthy();\r\n  expect(complete).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\r\n});\r\n\r\nit(\'renders each named opening method before the learner continues to guided practice\', () => {\r\n  for (const id of [\'orientation\', \'sufficient-necessary\', \'group-1\', \'group-2\']) {\r\n    const unit = conditionalUnits.find(item => item.id === id)!;\r\n    const instructions = `${unit.method.join(\' \')} ${unit.guidedExample.prompt}`;\r\n    const { unmount } = mount(id);\r\n    const guided = screen.getByRole(\'link\', { name: /Continue to guided practice/ });\r\n    for (const name of [\'What Test\', \'Personalized If/Then\']) {\r\n      if (!instructions.includes(name)) continue;\r\n      const method = screen.getByText(name, { selector: \'strong\' });\r\n      expect(method.compareDocumentPosition(guided) & Node.DOCUMENT_POSITION_FOLLOWING).toBeTruthy();\r\n    }\r\n    unmount();\r\n  }\r\n});\r\n\r\nit(\'gives each independently assigned group its own What Test explanation before target selection\', () => {\r\n  for (const id of [\'group-1\', \'group-2\']) {\r\n    const { unmount } = mount(id);\r\n    const method = screen.getByText(\'What Test\', { selector: \'strong\' });\r\n    const lab = screen.getByText(\'Read the whole idea\').closest<HTMLElement>(\'.co-lab\')!;\r\n    expect(method.closest(\'p\')?.textContent).toContain("The question helps you find the indicator\'s complete target");\r\n    expect(method.compareDocumentPosition(lab) & Node.DOCUMENT_POSITION_FOLLOWING).toBeTruthy();\r\n    unmount();\r\n  }\r\n});\r\n\r\nit(\'pauses on both directions of a Personalized If/Then instead of supplying a conclusion immediately\', () => {\r\n  const { complete } = mount(\'sufficient-necessary\');\r\n  const section = screen.getByRole(\'heading\', { name: \'Try the sentence from the inside\' }).closest<HTMLElement>(\'section\')!;\r\n  const pauses = within(section).getAllByRole(\'button\', { name: \'Show the reasoning\' });\r\n  expect(pauses).toHaveLength(2);\r\n  expect(within(section).queryByText(/It has been packed. Every order marked ready is packed/)).toBeNull();\r\n  expect(within(section).queryByText(/My order could be packed without a ready label/)).toBeNull();\r\n  fireEvent.click(pauses[0]);\r\n  expect(within(section).getByText(/It has been packed. Every order marked ready is packed/)).toBeTruthy();\r\n  expect(within(section).queryByText(/My order could be packed without a ready label/)).toBeNull();\r\n  fireEvent.click(pauses[1]);\r\n  expect(within(section).getByText(/My order could be packed without a ready label/)).toBeTruthy();\r\n  expect(complete).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\r\n});\r\n\r\nit(\'reuses the Personalized If/Then in Only if with a genuine check of the reverse\', () => {\r\n  const { container, complete } = mount(\'group-2\');\r\n  const section = container.querySelector<HTMLElement>(\'#co-only-if-a-condition-that-must-be-met\')!;\r\n  expect(within(section).getByText(\'Personalized If/Then\', { selector: \'strong\' })).toBeTruthy();\r\n  const pauses = within(section).getAllByRole(\'button\', { name: \'Show the reasoning\' });\r\n  expect(pauses).toHaveLength(2);\r\n  fireEvent.click(pauses[0]);\r\n  expect(within(section).getByText(/I\'ve completed it. The sentence requires project completion/)).toBeTruthy();\r\n  expect(within(section).queryByText(/I could complete the project without graduating/)).toBeNull();\r\n  fireEvent.click(pauses[1]);\r\n  expect(within(section).getByText(/I could complete the project without graduating/)).toBeTruthy();\r\n  expect(complete).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\r\n});\r\n\r\nit(\'renders the opening repair anchors with the assigned unit’s actual mistake and correction\', () => {\r\n  for (const id of [\'orientation\', \'sufficient-necessary\', \'group-1\', \'group-2\']) {\r\n    const unit = conditionalUnits.find(item => item.id === id)!;\r\n    const { container, unmount } = mount(id);\r\n    const repair = container.querySelector<HTMLElement>(\'section#repair\')!;\r\n    expect(repair).toBeTruthy();\r\n    expect(container.querySelectorAll(\'#repair\')).toHaveLength(1);\r\n    expect(repair.querySelector(\'h2\')?.textContent).toBeTruthy();\r\n    expect(within(repair).getByText(unit.commonMistake.mistake)).toBeTruthy();\r\n    expect(within(repair).getByText(unit.commonMistake.repair)).toBeTruthy();\r\n    unmount();\r\n  }\r\n});\r\n\r\nit(\'makes the invitation check distinguish the stated requirement from its tempting reverse\', () => {\r\n  const { complete } = mount(\'sufficient-necessary\');\r\n  const check = screen.getByRole(\'heading\', { name: \'Every finalist receives an invitation. Which requirement does this sentence establish?\' }).closest<HTMLElement>(\'.co-check\')!;\r\n  fireEvent.click(within(check).getByRole(\'button\', { name: \'Being a finalist is necessary for receiving an invitation.\' }));\r\n  expect(within(check).getByText(/You reversed the requirement/)).toBeTruthy();\r\n  expect(within(check).getByText(/allows someone who isn\'t a finalist to receive an invitation too/)).toBeTruthy();\r\n  fireEvent.click(within(check).getByRole(\'button\', { name: \'Receiving an invitation is necessary for being a finalist.\' }));\r\n  expect(within(check).getByText(/receiving one required for finalist status/)).toBeTruthy();\r\n  expect(complete).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\r\n});\r\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '# CLAUDE.md\r\n\r\nThis file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.\r\n\r\n## Project snapshot\r\n\r\nLSAT U is a premium ed-tech web app: React 18 + Vite 5 + TypeScript + Tailwind + shadcn/ui, Supabase backend with Edge Functions, hosted on Lovable. The Lovable-connected project is the source of truth; GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`) is the mirror. Treat the connected project as canonical unless told otherwise.\r\n\r\n## Second brain (product memory)\r\n\r\nProduct context, design decisions, pedagogy, and status do NOT live in this repo. They live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`. This repo is the code; the vault is the decision layer. Read the vault when starting a session, needing product/design/pedagogy context, or making a decision that touches roadmap or UX. Skip it for trivial code edits.\r\n\r\nPath sanity check first:\r\n\r\n- Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`\r\n- Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`\r\n\r\nIf any older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat that as stale — the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.\r\n\r\nProtocol is canonical in `~/ai-os/CLAUDE.md` (Session Start / Session End), run via two skills:\r\n\r\n- **`/pickup`** (start / "brief me"): reads `wiki/tasks.md` → `wiki/hot.md` → `wiki/dashboard.md` → `wiki/index.md` → latest `wiki/log.md` entry, then gives a 6-8 line briefing (Now / Next / open threads / suggested next) and waits.\r\n- **`/wrapup`** (handoff / "done for today"): proposes which tasks are done (confirm first), strikes them through in `wiki/tasks.md`, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, appends a dated `wiki/log.md` entry, updates the task anchor, gives a 4-line confirmation.\r\n\r\n`wiki/tasks.md` is the living ledger of Now / Next / Done; finished work is struck through with a date so a new session never re-runs it.\r\n\r\nWhere LSATU truth lives in the vault (paths relative to `~/ai-os`):\r\n\r\nThe vault was rebuilt on 2026-07-16. **Live** pages:\r\n\r\n- Teaching voice (route ALL student-facing copy through it): `wiki/teaching-voice.md`\r\n- The tutoring business (offers, channels, priorities): `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`\r\n- Hubs: `wiki/tasks.md`, `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/log.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, `wiki/index.md`\r\n- A student\'s whole record (transcripts, notes, homework): `students/<name>/` — see `students/README.md` for the primary/mirror rule\r\n- LSAT teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md` (fills by ingest)\r\n\r\n**Archived** — the pre-rebuild product knowledge is read-only reference at `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`; it is not maintained and may be stale. Re-ingest a page through `raw/` if it becomes load-bearing again rather than editing it in place:\r\n\r\n- Product context: `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md` (flagship Main Conclusion bootcamp)\r\n- Design system (colors, type, role tokens): `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`\r\n- Code/source map: `…/synthesis/lsatu-source-map.md` (regenerable from this codebase)\r\n- Quality bar all bootcamps finish to: `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`\r\n- Concepts, entities, domain index: `…/concepts/`, `…/entities/`, `…/index.md`\r\n\r\nRules: `raw/` is immutable input, never edit it. Read knowledge from `wiki/`, not `raw/`. Use the `obsidian` CLI (`search`, `backlinks`, `read`) for graph traversal (needs the Obsidian app open); flat file reads work without it. When saving deliverables to the vault, use `[[wiki-link]]` syntax and `UPPERCASE-HYPHENATED.md` naming.\r\n\r\n## Commands\r\n\r\nScripts (from `package.json`):\r\n\r\n- `npm run dev` — Vite dev server on port 8080\r\n- `npm run build` — production build\r\n- `npm run build:dev` — build with development mode flags\r\n- `npm run preview` — serve the built bundle\r\n- `npm run lint` — ESLint flat config (`eslint.config.js`)\r\n- `npm run test` — Vitest single run (runner wired, no specs exist yet)\r\n- `npm run test:watch` — Vitest watch mode\r\n- Single test file: `npx vitest run path/to/file.test.ts`\r\n\r\nPackage manager: **npm**. `package-lock.json` is canonical. A stray `bun.lock` / `bun.lockb` exists in the repo — ignore it, do not switch tooling.\r\n\r\n- `npm run typecheck` — `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json`. Not optional before a push: `vite build` uses esbuild, which strips types without checking them, so a type error ships and auto-deploys. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on this.\r\n- `npm run test:e2e` / `npm run playwright` — Playwright e2e (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).\r\n\r\nNo Prettier. Formatting is ESLint-only.\r\n\r\n## Architecture (big picture)\r\n\r\n- **Routing** — React Router v6. Routes are registered in `src/App.tsx`, pages live in `src/pages/`. `/foyer` is the landing surface. Feature-gated pages are wrapped by `ProtectedRoute`.\r\n- **Auth + permissions** — Supabase Auth session plus Lovable Cloud Auth JWT fallback (`@lovable.dev/cloud-auth-js`). Per-feature access flags (`has_practice_access`, `has_drill_access`, `has_chat_access`, `is_admin`, ...) are resolved in `src/hooks/useUserPermissions.ts`. Session death is detected via JWT/401.\r\n- **Supabase integration** — client auto-generated at `src/integrations/supabase/client.ts` (Supabase JS v2). DB types are auto-generated — do not hand-edit. Edge Functions (Deno) in `supabase/functions/`: `admin-manage-users`, `analyze-stem-response`, `generate-micro-drill`, `motivation-engine`, `parse-drill-request`, `tutor-chat`, `voice-coach-respond`. SQL migrations in `supabase/migrations/`.\r\n- **State** — React Context for cross-cutting concerns (`AuthContext`, `UserSettingsContext`, `QuestionBankContext`, `ThemeContext`, `TimerContext`). TanStack Query v5 for server state.\r\n- **Primary navigation — Foyer** — `src/pages/AcademyFoyer.tsx` composes `src/components/foyer/FoyerSidebar.tsx` (state blocks + workspace nav) with `src/components/foyer/FoyerHeroRing.tsx` (3 nodes: Smart Drill, Resume, Ask Joshua). This is the primary nav for the product.\r\n- **Messaging / Inbox** — real messaging feature, not cosmetic. Lives in `src/components/inbox/` (`FloatingMessenger`, `ConversationView`, `ThreadList`) plus `src/hooks/useInbox.ts`. One ongoing 1:1 thread per student with the admin (Joshua). Supports PDF attachments. Gated by `has_chat_access`.\r\n- **Practice surfaces** — Drill and Wrong Answer Journal (WAJ) are real product surfaces, not launchers. Core logic in `src/lib/`: `adaptiveEngine`, `drillIntelligence`, `achievementEngine`, `gamification`, `questionLoader`, `streakSystem`, `wajService`.\r\n- **Admin** — `AdminDashboard` + `admin-manage-users` edge function. Must be desktop AND mobile responsive. Needs granular per-feature toggle control.\r\n- **UI primitives** — shadcn/ui (Radix + `class-variance-authority`) in `src/components/ui/`. Icons from `lucide-react` and `@tabler/icons-react`. Framer Motion for animation. Spline for 3D.\r\n- **Styling** — Tailwind 3.4 over HSL custom properties in `src/index.css`, surfaced as tokens in `tailwind.config.ts`. `DESIGN.md` is the visual authority; `.impeccable/design.json` is its machine-readable sidecar. Prefer tokens over ad-hoc hex values, and never add a token name whose CSS variable is not defined - three such names (`accent-bronze`, `accent-warm`, `glow-bronze`) shipped broken for five months and silently removed the focus ring from every shared Button.\r\n\r\n## Conventions\r\n\r\n- **Git workflow:** Always commit and push directly to `main`. Never create feature branches or pull requests unless explicitly asked. After completing any code change: stage relevant files, commit with a descriptive message, and push to `main`.\r\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session; a raw push can get rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges origin/main if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes.\r\n- **Never roll `main` back.** Do not use Lovable\'s "Revert to commit", "revert and resend", or history restore, and never reset or force-push `main`. On 2026-09-27 an accidental "revert and resend" (b998596) silently undid three merged PRs across 121 question-bank files, and students saw the wrong questions again. To undo your own work, `git revert <your SHA>` only. If a Lovable dialog offers a revert, dismiss it; if one fires, stop and tell Joshua before repairing anything.\r\n- Path alias `@/` → `./src/` (set in `vite.config.ts` and `tsconfig.json`).\r\n- TypeScript strict mode is **off** (`strictNullChecks: false`, `noImplicitAny: false`, `noUnusedLocals: false`). Do not flip these without explicit approval — it would cascade into a very large diff.\r\n- Lovable-specific plumbing that must not be removed:\r\n  - `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin injects `data-lov-id` in dev\r\n  - `vite-plugin-pwa` configured with start URL `/foyer`\r\n  - Lovable preview Service Worker guard in `src/main.tsx`\r\n- Identity rules: email sign-in, profile identity uses **name** (not username), instructor is displayed as **"Joshua"**, students display their real profile names.\r\n\r\n## Cross-tool instruction hygiene\r\n\r\n`CLAUDE.md` is the fuller project source of truth. `AGENTS.md` is the Codex-facing adapter and should stay aligned without becoming a second full copy.\r\n\r\n- When updating project instructions, update both files as needed: put durable detail here, and keep `AGENTS.md` as a concise adapter.\r\n- When creating a Claude skill that Codex should use, also copy it to `.agents/skills/` with its `SKILL.md` and supporting files.\r\n- When creating a Claude agent that Codex should spawn, also create a matching `.codex/agents/<name>.toml` and place the converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.\r\n- When adding shared knowledge, put it in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/` so every tool can read the same source.\r\n\r\n## Verification (must not skip)\r\n\r\nAny change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not "done" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + browser tool, e.g. Playwright or chrome devtools MCP), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Do not report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This has been the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions (repeated "it\'s not working" / "i dont see it" corrections) — treat it as a hard gate, not a suggestion.\r\n\r\n**Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** That means narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A responsive defect that gets pushed as "fixed" and does not hold costs a second full session — this has already happened (a pushed Home-page fix that did not survive, then 13 screenshots and 8 emulate cycles to actually close it). One matrix pass before the push is cheaper than either.\r\n\r\n## Product rules (must not regress)\r\n\r\n- Foyer (`FoyerSidebar` + `FoyerHeroRing`) is the primary navigation. The Foyer additionally hosts a `GooeyDock` quick-launch row well below the orbital ring; this dock is allowed only on `/foyer`. Do not add docks elsewhere.\r\n- Inbox is real messaging with back-and-forth threads and PDF attachments, scoped 1:1 per student with admin.\r\n- Admin is fully responsive on desktop and mobile.\r\n- Do not expose actions users cannot actually perform — no fake analytics, no dead buttons, no misleading placeholders. Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake data.\r\n- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email.\r\n\r\n## Known gaps (do not invent answers)\r\n\r\n- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run them with `npm run test`. Playwright is wired with one e2e spec (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), run via `npm run test:e2e`.\r\n- No Prettier config and no CI workflows. Nothing runs `typecheck`, `lint`, or the tests automatically — `.claude/safe-push.sh` is the only gate, and only for typecheck.\r\n- `README.md` is a stub.\r\n- `.claude/settings.json` is set up with a permission allowlist for safe dev work (npm scripts, git reads, MCP GitHub reads) and a denylist for destructive ops. No custom `.claude/agents/`. A `SessionStart` auto-pull hook is wired in `.claude/settings.local.json` (runs `.claude/auto-pull-hook.sh`); Codex mirrors it in `.codex/hooks.json`.\r\n\r\n## Skill / agent / command vault\r\n\r\nThe full skill/agent/command vault lives in the brain root at `~/ai-os/skills/`, `~/ai-os/agents/`, `~/ai-os/commands/` (split into `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees, indexed by `~/ai-os/skills/INDEX.md`). It is inert storage — nothing here auto-loads. To use a vault skill in this project, symlink it into `.claude/skills/` (Codex: `.agents/skills/`). When you create a new skill/agent/command, save it to BOTH this project\'s `.claude/<kind>/` and the vault, so the vault stays the complete catalog.\r\n\r\n## Agent skills\r\n\r\n### Issue tracker\r\n\r\nGitHub Issues on `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`, via the `gh` CLI. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\r\n\r\n### Triage labels\r\n\r\nThe five canonical roles, unchanged: `needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\r\n\r\n### Domain docs\r\n\r\nMulti-context: `CONTEXT-MAP.md` points at eight per-context glossaries; ADRs are all at the root. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.\r\n'}]

Your reverse arrow rules that out: it says every ring proves the countdown reached zero. The sentence only guarantees a ring when the countdown reaches zero.\",\n\"An email sent without a confirmed reservation has not been ruled out. Starting with the email would make confirmation certain, but the sentence only tells us what confirmed reservations receive.\",\n\"A guardrail beside a gentle slope would fit the sentence too. Guardrail → Steep drop would exclude that possibility. The original rule tells us about steep drops, not every place a guardrail can stand.\",\n\"A lidded mug from another display would fit this rule. Your arrow would put that mug in the blue display. The sentence tells us what blue-display mugs come with; it hasn't restricted lids to them.\",\n\"A vegetarian recipe could be missing from the booklet. Nothing here requires every vegetarian recipe to be printed. Your reverse arrow adds that promise of a place in the booklet.\",\n\"A jacket hanging in the back closet doesn't break this rule. A hanger proves neither closet. Starting from the hanger would make the front-closet location certain without that connection.\",\n\"A sandwich ordered with cheese could also get a green wrapper. The sentence hasn't reserved those wrappers for cheese-free orders. Your arrow makes that restriction even though the original doesn't.\",\n\"A guest could have a booking without receiving a key. That meets the requirement without establishing the result. Booking → Receives key would promise a key to every booked guest.\",\n\"An approved job could remain unreleased. The rule requires approval for release; it doesn't guarantee release for every approved job. Your arrow would make approval enough.\",\n\"The pump could be on while the fountain isn't running. That fits the stated requirement. Your arrow would exclude it by turning the pump's switched-on state into a guarantee of running.\",\n\"Parking could be permitted somewhere the van never stops. The sentence hasn't promised a stop at every permitted location. Starting from permission would add exactly that promise.\",\n\"An unreserved desk could also have a name tag. That leaves every reserved desk tagged, as the rule requires. Your reverse arrow would make every tag proof of a reservation.\",\n\"Someone could sign a checkout form without borrowing the telescope. The sentence hasn't ruled that out. Your arrow would turn the signed form into proof of borrowing instead of keeping it a requirement.\",\n\"A visitor in another room could have the same safety-glasses obligation. The sentence doesn't restrict that obligation to workshop users. Your arrow would make the obligation proof that the visitor uses the workshop.\"\n]\npatterns=list(re.finditer(r'\"reversedPromise\": \"[^\"\\n]*\"',block))\nassert len(patterns)==len(reverses)==14\nfor m,value in reversed(list(zip(patterns,reverses))):\n    block=block[:m.start()]+'\"reversedPromise\": '+json.dumps(value)+block[m.end():]\ns=s[:start]+block+s[end:]\ns=s.replace('whenever the kitchen timer reaches zero.','whenever its countdown reaches zero.')\ns=s.replace('But the first rule alone may not.','But this rule alone doesn\\'t.')\np.write_text(s,encoding='utf-8')\n'@ | py -","workdir":"C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-conditional-bootcamp","max_output_tokens":1000})).output);

return; }\n+    const update = () => {\n+      if (!figure.current || !indicator.current || !target.current) return;\n+      const origin = figure.current.getBoundingClientRect();\n+      const firstRects = indicator.current.getClientRects();\n+      const targetRects = target.current.getClientRects();\n+      const first = firstRects[firstRects.length - 1];\n+      const last = targetRects[targetRects.length - 1];\n+      if (!first?.width || !last?.width) return;\n+      const x1 = (first.left + first.right) / 2 - origin.left;\n+      const x2 = (last.left + last.right) / 2 - origin.left;\n+      const y1 = first.bottom - origin.top + 5;\n+      const y2 = last.bottom - origin.top + 5;\n+      const baseline = Math.max(y1, y2) + 28;\n+      setConnector(`M ${x1} ${y1} V ${baseline} H ${x2} V ${y2}`);\n+    };\n+    update();\n+    const observer = typeof ResizeObserver === 'undefined' ? null : new ResizeObserver(update);\n+    if (figure.current) observer?.observe(figure.current);\n+    window.addEventListener('resize', update);\n+    return () => { observer?.disconnect(); 1) {\n+      fireEvent.click(within(lab).getByRole('button', { name: 'Next sentence' }));\n+      expect(lab.querySelector('mark')).toBeNull();\n+    }\n+  }\n+});\n+\n+it('keeps each hero specific to its lesson and gives group recall a single sentence-to-rule job', () => {\n+  const captions = new Set<string>();\n+  for (const id of ['orientation', 'sufficient-necessary', 'group-1', 'group-2']) {\n+    const { container, unmount } = mount(id);\n+    const figure = container.querySelector<HTMLElement>('header .co-hero-figure')!;\n+    expect(figure).toBeTruthy();\n+    expect(figure.querySelector('figcaption')?.textContent).toBeTruthy();\n+    captions.add(figure.querySelector('figcaption')!.textContent!);\n+    expect(container.querySelector('.co-hero-orbit')).toBeNull();\n+    if (id === 'orientation') {\n+      expect(figure.textContent).not.toMatch(/ready|packed|→/i);\n+    }\n+    if (id.startsWith('group-')) {\n+      expect(container.querySelectorAll('.co-flash')).toHaveLength(1);\n+      expect(container.querySelector('#co-return-to-the-relationship')).toBeNull();\n+    }\n+    expect(screen.queryByText('Think it through before revealing. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 19.7 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"21af57","wall_time_seconds":10.0129924,"session_id":82027,"original_token_count":19,"output":"\\n RUN  v3.2.4 C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-conditional-bootcamp\\n\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': "> With one short rule, you may not need to. When a passage gives you several relationships, a diagram lets you compare them without rereading the wording each time. In this exercise, write the relationship so we can check that you kept its direction and complete ideas.\r\n\r\nThe answer gives a decision criterion and distinguishes the purpose of this exercise from a blanket rule about every test question.\r\n\r\n### Transfer the voice to another LSAT topic\r\n\r\n> A conclusion is the claim the author is asking you to accept. The other statements are supposed to support it.\r\n>\r\n> Nora says, “The library should stay open later because every evening seat was taken this week.” What is she trying to persuade us of?\r\n>\r\n> That the library should stay open later. The full seats are her reason. She expects that reason to support the proposed change.\r\n>\r\n> You don't have to agree with her. First, separate what she wants you to accept from what she gives you in support.\r\n\r\nThe same mechanism works outside conditionals: definition, small example, question, explanation, and a useful boundary. No conditional topic has been imported into the conclusion lesson.\r\n\r\n## Revise the draft in a useful order\r\n\r\n### First, check the reasoning and scope\r\n\r\nList what is given, what follows, and why. Try to construct a case that satisfies the given information but makes the proposed conclusion false. If that case is possible, revise the logic before polishing the voice. Check modality, negation, quantifier scope, and whether the learner has actually been taught every required operation.\r\n\r\nFor a rewrite, preserve Joshua's established method and explicit lesson scope. The new reference governs delivery. It does not authorize removing content that students need or adding source topics the current assignment does not cover.\r\n\r\n### Then, check the learner's experience\r\n\r\nRead as someone who has never seen the notation. Can they tell what the concept is? Can they answer the first question with what the page has taught? Does each new term refer to an idea they have seen? Does the page tell them why the correct answer follows?\r\n\r\nPut the explanation beside the point where it is needed. Do not rely on an interaction to imply a distinction the prose never explains. A student who cannot use the animation must still be able to learn from the text.\r\n\r\n### Then, listen to the voice\r\n\r\nRead aloud at an ordinary teaching pace. Replace stiff phrasing with the words the teacher would naturally use. Keep the intermediate thoughts. Vary sentence length where the task changes. Remove repeated catchphrases, inflated enthusiasm, branded metaphors, and irrelevant asides.\r\n\r\nDo not repair generic prose by scattering “Cool,” “Let's,” or “That's it” through it. Repair the missing reasoning, the abrupt transition, or the abstract example.\r\n\r\n### Finally, verify the student surface\r\n\r\nCheck the actual prompt, answer state, labels, buttons, diagrams, and explanations. A good note can be undermined by a shell that exposes internal planning or reveals the answer too soon. Preview desktop and mobile when the interface changes. Keep the instructor's planning map separate from what the learner sees.\r\n\r\n## Use this rubric before handing over LSAT writing\r\n\r\nScore each dimension from 0 to 2: **0** absent or misleading; **1** partly present but needing revision; **2** clear and usable. This is an editorial tool, not a validated learning measure. A logical error or scope violation requires revision regardless of the total.\r\n\r\n| Dimension | What a strong draft does |\r\n|---|---|\r\n| **Ordinary meaning** | Defines the idea in language the reader can understand before notation. |\r\n| **Concrete anchor** | Uses an original, small, unambiguous situation and states needed assumptions. |\r\n| **Visible reasoning** | Shows the intermediate connection instead of asserting a result. |\r\n| **Point of view** | Keeps the reader involved; uses teacher narration purposefully. |\r\n| **Cadence** | Gives complex thoughts room and short conclusions a clear place to land. |\r\n| **Vocabulary** | Keeps useful technical terms, explains them, and avoids internal jargon. |\r\n| **Questions and pauses** | Asks a genuine decision and gives an appropriate chance to answer. |\r\n| **Boundaries** | Shows what the information leaves open and why a tempting move fails. |\r\n| **Progression** | Each operation has a prerequisite, a reason to appear, and adequate practice. |\r\n| **Transfer** | Fresh questions require the taught skill, with reasoned feedback. |\r\n| **Tone and warmth** | Expects effort, supports the next action, and never mocks uncertainty. |\r\n| **Presentation** | Examples, comparisons, notation, and interactions clarify the reasoning. |\r\n\r\nDo not treat a high total as proof of an identical voice. Use the original source and Joshua's response to calibrate the subjective feel. His concrete corrections override a score.\r\n\r\n## A reusable writing brief\r\n\r\nUse this when giving another writer or agent the task. Supply the actual source, lesson scope, prerequisite knowledge, and approved method along with it.\r\n\r\n```text\r\nWrite this LSAT material in Joshua's selected teaching voice.\r\nRead wiki/teaching-voice.md, references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md,\r\nand wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md before drafting.\r\n\r\nKeep the reader beside the teacher. Start with the ordinary meaning of\r\nthe idea, then make it concrete with an original, intuitive example.\r\nShow each small inference in natural language. Ask the question a learner\r\nwould actually need to answer here. When the question is practice, leave\r\nroom for an attempt before revealing the reasoning.\r\n\r\nVary sentence length by purpose. Let explanations breathe and use short\r\nsentences to settle a consequence or a limit. Use contractions naturally.\r\nTeach the technical term when there is a clear idea to attach it to.\r\nRepeat an anchor only when the repetition changes the learner's view.\r\nShow a tempting mistake and how to detect it when that distinction matters.\r\n\r\nPreserve Joshua's method, the exact logical strength, complete conditions,\r\nand the selected topic scope. Give fresh practice with explained answers.\r\nDo not copy the reference's examples, jokes, personal claims, or procedural\r\ncounts. No em dashes, hype, invented evidence, internal stage language,\r\nor decorative catchphrases. Light humor is optional and must help the lesson.\r\n\r\nRead the result aloud, check every inference, and review the student-facing\r\nprompt and answer states. If the reader cannot hear why the next thought\r\nfollows, revise the explanation before polishing the surface.\r\n```\r\n\r\n## Keep this reference current\r\n\r\nThis file is the detailed method reference, not a second canonical brand voice. [Teaching voice](../wiki/teaching-voice.md) remains the router. [COPY-CORRECTIONS](../wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md) remains the record of demonstrated wording failures. Add Joshua's later calibration to the relevant section here rather than creating competing LSAT voice documents.\r\n\r\nThe project-owned `lsat-u-voice` skill reads this standard. Recap and bootcamp workflows also read it explicitly. An older vendor voice skill describes a different writer blend; it must not override this Joshua-selected standard in this project. Managed plugin caches are preserved rather than silently rewritten.\r\n\r\nThe source archive is immutable. Future evidence or revised attachments should be recorded as a separate generation with provenance, not written over the original.\r\n"}]
